| `MvPolynomial.hasseCoeff` | `MvPolynomial.taylor` | no | A: Defines the coordinatewise binomial coefficient for two finitely supported natural multi-indices, multiplying only over the differentiation index support. Mechanism: Uses Finsupp.prod with Nat.choose; omitted coordinates contribute choose(k,0)=1, so the finite-support presentation gives the full multivariate binomial factor. B: Defines multivariate polynomial translation by a vector as a bundled coefficient-algebra endomorphism. Mechanism: The universal evaluation homomorphism aeval substitutes X j+C(α j) for each variable, automatically preserving sums, products, and coefficients. Hasse differentiation is coefficient-linear exponent lowering by a chosen multi-index, whereas Taylor is a multiplicative substitution by X+α. Their substantive bridge is coeff_taylor: translation packages all evaluated Hasse derivatives as coefficients. It is not equality of the two operators. Both have zero/composition/map/rename laws, but their composition parameters differ (binomial-scaled addition of orders versus unscaled addition of translation vectors). Ring inverse translation is not available for general semirings and factorial division is not valid in arbitrary characteristic. | Retain the distinct conclusions; coordinate common monomial/finite-support/substitution primitives through their already reviewed APIs. No new pair-specific parametric theorem is justified by these different outputs. |
